| Q7 | Stars on pupil pages | No (teacher-side only) | Fade levels | Print ★ after band label |
| Q8 | Exit ticket as a 2-up slip with "I can do this." / "I need one more day." | Yes | §9.7 | Full-page exit ticket (no reflection) |
| Q9 | SAY rule without italics (quotes + 13 pt + label); tab ids `Teacher n` | Yes | §10.3, §8.7 | Self-host Andika Italic for teacher pages |
| Q10 | Keep "circle the mistake in a sequence" as count-row production items | Yes | Bank rule 4 | Delete them |
| extra | One-symbol answers up to 15–16 per page | Settled by the owner's revised OR-b (2026-10-16): counts follow the capacity rules | §8.11, §8.12.1 | — |

### 8.14 Abbreviations — Preferred Usage Rule

**Default: use the full term.** Only use an abbreviation when it genuinely
helps readability — for example, when a term has already appeared many times
on the same page, when space in a table cell is tight, or when the short form
is universally understood by teachers (e.g. "MWBs" is fine after the first
introduction).

**Do not scatter abbreviations for their own sake.** If writing "mini
whiteboards" or "English language learners" reads naturally in context, write
it out. The goal is clear, readable teacher language — not economy of
characters.

**Introduction rule:** Each term must be introduced in full at least once on
any page where the abbreviation is then used. All five are defined in the
Reference Key (T2b), but each delivery page is read in isolation, so if a term
first appears on T4, introduce it in full there too.

| Abbreviation | Full form | When abbreviation is acceptable |
|---|---|---|
| MWBs | mini whiteboards | After first full use on the page; inside table cells |
| ELL | English language learners | After first full use on the page; inside table cells |
| SPED | special education students | After first full use on the page; inside table cells |
| RCDWS | Read, Circle, Draw, Write, Solve | After first full use on the page; inside table cells |
| CRA | Concrete-Representational-Abstract | After first full use on the page; inside table cells |


### 8.15 No workspace on teacher materials (CRITICAL)
Zero blank workspace, zero empty boxes, zero answer lines on teacher pages (the check boxes of the
`Materials:` list are the one exception: they are a teacher's tick list). Board-space references appear as
one text line with the black `board` icon: `Board: teacher models here` (sentence case, no capitals, no
brackets).

### 8.16 Delivery Page Script Format (MANDATORY — applies to all of T3, T4, T5) [T-SCRIPT]

Every action in every delivery section uses this format.
**The teacher's eye must be able to find the spoken words instantly.**

```
 (3)  Do:    What the teacher physically does. One sentence. Action only.
             e.g. "Write Example 1 on the board — no speaking yet."
      Say:   “Exact words to speak.”            <- 13 pt, inside curly quotes
      Ask:   “A question to put to students.”   <- 13 pt, inside curly quotes
      Note:  A tip, a warning, what to look for, or the expected answer (in the key colour).
------------------------------------------------------------------------------  0.75 pt hairline
```

**The spoken-words rule (replaces V20's italic rule).** Andika is loaded upright only, so italics would
silently print upright. Spoken words are found by three cues instead: the bold `Say:` / `Ask:` label, the
curly quotes “…”, and the larger size (13 pt against the 11 pt body). **Owner default (2026-10-16,
recommended; may change) — Q9.** Flip: self-host Andika Italic for teacher pages only and set spoken
words in italic as well.

**Layout of a step row.** A grid of three columns: the step numeral in a 6.4 mm accent circle (the same
mark as the pupil `Steps:`, so "step 3" looks the same to teacher and pupil), a 14 mm label column
(`Do:` `Say:` `Ask:` `Note:` bold 11 pt), then the text. Rows are divided by 0.75 pt hairlines. No shaded
bands, no coloured borders.

**Four labels only:**

| Label | What it is | Format |
|---|---|---|
| **Do:** | Physical action — write, point, distribute, wait, walk | 11 pt regular |
| **Say:** | Words to speak to the whole class | 13 pt, in curly quotes |
| **Ask:** | A question for students | 13 pt, in curly quotes; offer a point / gesture / one-word answer |
| **Note:** | Tip, what to look for, maths explanation, expected answers | 11 pt; expected answers in #C2410C bold |

**CHECK is not a separate label.** What to look for belongs inside the Note line.
Note lines appear only where a teacher might genuinely freeze or needs context —
not after every step.

**Numbered steps:** Every delivery section uses numbered steps (1, 2, 3…).
The teacher should never ask "what do I do now?" — the answer is always the next number.

**Language rules (MANDATORY for all of T3–T5):**
- Plain English only. No theory jargon.
  "Show every step clearly" — not "overtise."
  "Take the hints away when ready" — not "covertisation."
  "Wrong example" — not "non-example."
  "Most students got it" — not "80% success rate."
- Every `Say:` / `Ask:` sentence is **12 words or fewer** [P-LG-12].
- Data loop labels: "Fewer than half" / "About half" / "Most" — never percentages (§10.6).
- Explain any maths that might trip up a non-specialist. If an answer is
  non-obvious, say why it is correct.
- Flow is linear. Each numbered step leads to the next. The only branching
  is the three-option data loop after the Check Question.

---
## Part 9 — Pupil pages: the lesson packet (MANDATORY)

Every pupil page: A4 portrait (P6 landscape), size L, the frame of §8.7, black / white / grey plus the
accent on step marks, one I Can title, a facsimile key (§8.12.7). Geometry below is for A4; on Letter the
widths are identical and the body is 18 mm shorter (fewer rows when needed, never smaller type).

### 9.1 P0 Prerequisite Check [LR-17, AUD C-10]

Purpose: 3–4 questions, most basic first, one for each **Pre-requisite atom on T2**, each from ONE earlier
lesson, so the teacher can route pupils before teaching (§8.12.8). A question may hold two items of the same
kind when one item alone would leave an empty band (Score then counts items). Replaces the V20 warm-up (**Owner default Q3**).

```
| header 26 mm: Name / Date / Score /4 · tab Level n / strand / Prerequisite · title                    |
+======================================================================================================+
| Count. Write the number.                                    (key only: If missed -> Lesson K-3 ...)  | strip 8
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| a.        [picture, item 12 mm, rows of 5]                     [answer square 24 mm]                 | cell
+======================================================================================================+
| Write the missing number.                                                                            |
| b.            [ 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | [box] | 8 | 9 ]   number track, 18 x 16 mm boxes                       |
+======================================================================================================+
| ... c. ...  d. ...                                                                                   |
```
- One mini-section per question: its own band (strip with the library instruction, then one full-width
  cell). Heights follow content (flex weights, for example 1.15 / 0.85 / 0.8 / 1.55 for count / track /
  compare / bond), so no cell has an empty band ≥ 30 %; the four together fill the 236 mm body.
- Content in each cell is spread across the width (`justify-content: space-evenly`).
- Score `/N` = number of questions. Key: answers in slots + "If missed → Lesson …" in each strip; the
  routing table goes on T6.

### 9.2 P1 Number sense (optional, default on) [AUD C-11, Owner default Q4]

```
| header 26 mm · Score /10 · tab Number sense                                                          |
+======================================================================================================+
| Number sense: A                                                                                      | strip 8
|#1#|  Write 1 more and 1 less.   |        [box] 5 [box]     ("1 less" / "1 more" 12 pt under boxes)  | row
|#2#|  Write the missing number.  |        3, 4, [box], 6                                             |
| ... 5 rows ...                                                                                       |
+======================================================================================================+
| Number sense: B   (same five types, harder numbers, tabs 6-10)                                       |
```
- Daily look: cell borders 1.5 pt (shared), black number tabs 6 × 6 mm with white 11 pt numerals.
- Each row cell: tab; the item's library instruction at 15 pt in a 58 mm column (at most 2 lines); the
  item at 28 pt centred in the remaining width. Ten rows share the body equally (about 22 mm each).
- One type per row, the same five types in the same order in A and B. One box width per band (B(n) for
  the band's longest answer). Every answer is a production slot; no multiple choice.
- Items that need a picture taller than 16 mm (ten frame, bond) do not go on P1; use them on P0 or orally.

### 9.3 P2 Anchor chart (one page, the poster; the board copy) [LR-1, LR-15, PT-ANC, AUD C-16]

```
| lifted header 17 mm: title centred in row A + tab (Level n / strand / Anchor chart); no Name / Score   |
+======================================================================================================+
| [stack, step 1 state]        (1)[icon] Start with the big number.     <- 18 pt bold, accent marks    |
|   digits 34 pt                Start with 7.                           <- 15 pt                       |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| [stack, step 2 state]        (2)[icon] Count on.                                                     |
|   newest mark grey            Count on 2: 8, 9.   [hop track, newest numbers grey]                    |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| [stack, step 3 state]        (3)[icon] Write how many.                                               |
+======================================================================================================+
| Another example:                                                                                     |
| Big number second? Start with it.        |  Same numbers? Start with either.                         |
|   [final state, 30 pt]  [hops]           |    [final state, 30 pt]  [hops]                           |
+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Say:  “7 plus 2 equals 9.”                                                                           |  13 mm
+======================================================================================================+
| Rule:  ( Big number first. Count on. Write it. )   <- 18 pt bold in a 3 mm rounded 1.5 pt box         |
```
- One worked example in 2–5 **step panels** (one row each; drawing in the left 42 %, step head and words
  right). Each panel is the same item in the state that step produces; the mark the step adds is GREY
  (the newest mark), earlier marks black. The step head is: accent numeral in a 10 mm circle, the step
  icon (8 mm), the step name in 18 pt bold (2–5 words, a print verb first). Under it one 15 pt line.
- **Other examples** (`Another example:` band, or the last panel of a 2 × 3 panel grid, 1–3 cells): one
  final-state drawing for **every case the practice will deal that the worked example does not show** (here:
  big number second with a sum of 10; same numbers) [LR-1]. Each carries a one-line bold caption (≤ 8 words)
  and is drawn **all in black** with every step done, answer included (`factStack` state `'ink'`; grey would
  read "trace this" on a poster). The chart carries **no answer marks** (`.ka` / `data-ka`), so its key is
  identical to it apart from the tab (self-check E17b).
- **Every step panel adds at least one grey mark** — the newest mark that step makes (step 1 of a column
  subtraction: grey rings round the two ones digits; the check panel: the grey carry box and the grey answer
  strip). Earlier marks are black (self-check E26).
- Panel layout: 3 steps → one full-width row per step (drawing left 42 %); 4–5 steps → a 2 × 3 grid of panels,
  numeral + icon + step name on top, drawing, one 15 pt line under it, the 6th panel the other example.
- `Say:` band with the filled oral frame; `Rule:` band with the chant (≤ 8 words, said twice in the
  plenary).
- Poster sizing: drawings enlarge to fill the page (digits 34 pt in the panels, 30 pt in other examples);
  never below 0.8× / 0.85× of the size they would be elsewhere. No Name, no Score, no slots. Its key is the
  same chart with the tab `Answer Key` (every pupil page has one key).

### 9.4 P3 Lesson sheet (Vocabulary · Remember · Guided Practice + Steps · Independent Practice) [LR-4, D17]

```
| header 26 mm · Score /6 (Independent cells only) · tab Lesson 1                                      |
+======================================================================================================+
| Vocabulary:  Draw a line to match.                                                                   |
|      [+]                    [=]                    [7 8 9 hops]      <- mini-diagrams, dot under each|
|       .                      .                      .                                                |
|       .                      .                      .              <- 12 mm channel for the line     |
|     equals                count on                 plus             <- words 15 pt bold, shuffled    |
+======================================================================================================+
| Remember:  Start at the bigger number and count on.                                                  |
+======================================================================================================+
| Guided Practice:  Add.                                    | Steps:                                   |
| [cell 1: step 1 in grey] | [cell 2] | [cell 3]            | (1)[icon] Start with the big number.     |
|  hint scaffolds grey      unlabelled, unscored            | (2)[icon] Count on.                      |
|                                                           | (3)[icon] Write how many.   13 pt        |
+======================================================================================================+
| Independent Practice:  Add.                                                                          |
| a.                 | b.                 | c.                                                       |
| d.                 | e.                 | f.                                                       |
```
- Vocabulary: ≤ 3 terms, each a mini-diagram over a solid 2.5 mm dot, the shuffled words under dots;
  the dots face each other across a 12 mm channel (match with a line).
- `Remember:` one line, ≤ 12 words (optionally a "Remember … NOT …" contrast line, Appendix C.4).
- `Guided Practice:` 2–3 unlabelled cells carrying the chart's representation, **sized to their content**
  (stack + answer row + pads: about 51 mm for a vertical fact, 62 mm for a regrouping stack). **Cell 1 has
  step 1 done in grey and is the Model's case; the next cells are the other cases** (varied, never three of
  one case); the other hint scaffolds grey in every cell. `Steps:` panel 66 mm wide at the right (BD-4:
  the Steps sit beside the cells), the numerals and icons in the accent.
- `Independent Practice:` takes the remaining height in **whole rows of 3** lettered cells: add a row while
  every cell stays at least its content height (one-symbol facts ≈ 45 mm, regrouping stacks ≈ 64 mm) and no
  band ≥ 30 % is left (§8.9). Hint scaffolds off; structural ones stay. Score `/N` = the Independent count.

### 9.5 P4 Practice ×n (+ optional Mixed) [OR-b, D17, LR-8]

```
| header 26 mm · Score /12 · tab Practice 1                                                            |
| Steps: (1)[icon] Start with the big number. (2)[icon] Count on. (3)[icon] Write how many.  <- strip   |
| Add.                                                                                <- instruction 9 mm|
+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| a.                     | b.                      | c.                                              |
|        2               |         1               |        3        <- 28 pt, tracks 0.72 em          |
|    +   8               |     +   7               |    +   6                                         |
|    ------- 1.5 pt      |     -------             |    -------                                       |
|   (12 mm answer row, then the open work zone)                                                       |
```
- Step strip (12 pt text, accent numerals and icons, a 0.75 pt rule under it), then the instruction,
  then ONE framed grid. Count from §8.12.1 (revised OR-b): 6 (2 × 3) for a standard procedure (2-digit
  regrouping with its Check line: 2 × 3, as on the site), 4 (2 × 2) for long procedures, one-symbol answers up
  to 16 (vertical facts: 3 × 4 = 12).
- The last row holds the edge cases (★★★): for example doubles, the big number second, a sum of 10.
- A lesson prints 1–2 Practice pages (as many as the teacher asks). Each starts its letters at `a.`.
- Optional Mixed page: earlier skills only, the lesson skill fills at least half the items. It keeps the
  pack's I Can title (R5); the band label `Mixed Review:` names it; tab line 3 `Mixed n`.
- Item rules for every page of the packet [LR-2, LR-5…7]: only the cases the chart teaches; **no exact
  repeat in the packet** (chart examples included); **no turn-around pair on one page**; a chart example is
  never dealt turned round; no near twins; **no two items on a page share an answer** (when the pool has
  fewer distinct answers than the page has items — add 1–3 within 10 has 9 — none in one row); **the last row
  holds only edge cases** (that is what ★★★ means); at most one or two rare-case items in the other rows. **Pool test:** count the distinct items the skill allows. When the pool is
  smaller than the packet needs, turn-arounds may appear on later pages, and the exit slip may repeat Practice
  items — **only as many as the pool is short**: allowed repeats = places dealt − (pool size − forbidden
  items). Add 1–3 within 10: 41 places, 39 ordered facts, 2 forbidden (the chart examples 7 + 2 and 2 + 8
  turned round, LR-5) → 4 repeats allowed, all on the exit slip (Appendix E24).
- **The pool travels with the pack.** The builder writes the pool into `pack.html` as
  `<script type="application/json" id="v21-pool">{"op":"+","pool":[[a,b],…],"forbidden":[[a,b],…],"edge":[[a,b],…]}</script>`
  and marks every dealt item cell `data-item="a+b"` (or `a−b`) `data-row="r"` (the second exit-slip half is a
  copy and carries no marks). The self-check reads only these.

### 9.6 P5 Word problem (RCDWS panel) [AUD C-13, Owner default Q5]

```
| header 26 mm · Score /1 · tab Word problem                                                           |
| Read the story. Draw it. Solve.                                                                      |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------+
| ( story box: 3 mm radius, 1.5 pt; one sentence per line,  | [ ] [read]      Read it two times.       |
|   at most 4 lines, 15 pt, line height 1.45; fills the     | [ ] [ringnum]   Circle the numbers.      |
|   cell; the last line is the answer line:                 | [ ] [underline] Underline the question.  |
|   "Omar has ____ cars." )                                 | [ ] [draw]      Draw it.                 |
|                                                           | [ ] [write]     Write the equation.      |
|                                                           | [ ] [check]     Solve. Check.            |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------+
| Workspace:                                         (the Draw-it zone, >= 60 mm tall; takes spare)  |
|                                                                                                    |
+====================================================================================================+
|                      ____   ( )   ____   =   ____                    <- equation frame             |
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Say:  “The answer is ____ cars.”                                                                   |
```
- Top row 72 mm: story 58 %, routine panel 42 % (78 mm): **six check boxes, one action each, one line each**
  (7 mm box, accent line-art icon, the action at 13 pt — the Steps-box size — so each row fits on one line,
  row height ≤ 9 mm). The answer-with-unit line sits inside the story box as its last line (SF-50). Word bank (≤ 4 words) only on a Guided (scaffolded) form; v1 = 1 problem per page,
  v2 (faded: no panel, "Solve. Write the number and the label.") = 2 per page.
- Story rules: our own words (Q6), one sentence per line, ≤ 60 characters per line, the unit word in
  the story and pre-printed after the answer line, no idioms. The question is the last line.
- Flip of Q5: replace the panel with the schema diagram (part-whole bar model, Appendix D `barModel`).

### 9.7 P6 Exit slip (2-up half page) [AUD C-18, Owner default Q8]

```
A4 landscape, margins 12 / 13 / 12 mm, live 271 x 186 mm. Two identical halves, each 132.5 mm wide, 6 mm
apart, a dashed 0.75 pt cut line in the middle with the scissors glyph at its top. Each half:
| Name ________________   Score ____ /4                  [tab: Level n / strand / Exit slip] |
|                I Can add 1–3 (facts to 10)          (may wrap to 2 lines)                 |
|============================================================================================|
| Add.                                                                                       |
+---------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| a.  big number first                        | b.  big number second, count 3 (Critical Atom)|
+---------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| c.  same numbers                            | d.  sum of 10 (or: tens answer 0)             |
+---------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| Say:  “a. 4 plus 3 equals ____.”                                                           |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  [ ] I can do this.      [ ] I need one more day.
```
- **4 items (2 × 2), one per case the chart teaches; cells equal; no lone item.** The items include the
  Critical Atom's hardest case (here: big number second with a count of 3, which catches both "Starting
  small" and "Counting the start"). Each item and the mistake it detects is listed on T5 Section 10.
- Items are in the Practice cell format at 28 pt (stacks without T O heads), Score `/4`.
- One footer for the sheet. No reflection writing. The two check boxes are the pupil's own signal to the
  teacher, not a tracker [SC-6]. Flip of Q8: one full-width exit band of 4 items per page.

---
## Part 10 — Teacher pages: one family with the pupil pages (MANDATORY) [AUD §4]

Teacher pages look like the anchor chart and the keys: same frame, Andika, black, white and one grey,
scannable mid-lesson. A4 portrait, live area 186 mm, body 245 mm under the lifted 17 mm header.

### 10.1 T-HEAD and T-FOOT
- Header: no Name / Date / Score. The I Can title is lifted into row A beside the strand tab (17 mm).
  Tab: `Level n` / strand / `Teacher 1`…`Teacher 5`, `Reference` (T2b), `Answer Key` (T6 and keys).
  Continuation pages use the 12 mm header.
- Footer (7 pt): left `skill id · Grade n · CCSS · EE`; centre `n/N` (teacher pages counted together);
  right `Teacher · Form A · seed`. The © line under it. No "TEACHER USE ONLY" banner.

### 10.2 T-TYPE — see §8.3 (section heads 15 pt bold in 8 mm band strips; sub-heads 13 pt; body 11 pt;
spoken lines 13 pt; tables 10 pt).

### 10.3 T-SCRIPT — see §8.16 (accent step numeral · 14 mm label column · text; hairlines between steps).

### 10.4 Tables
0.75 pt rules under every row, head row Andika 700 under a 1.5 pt rule, no fills, no zebra rows, text
10 pt, numbers right-aligned. Atom types, step names and mistake names are **bold black sentence case**
(for example **Counting the start**), never blue, never ALL CAPS.

### 10.5 T-BOX-1…4 (embedded teaching and feedback boxes)

Each box: a 3 mm rounded container, padding 1.8 × 3 mm, a 5.8 mm black line-art icon in a left column,
then a bold label word with a colon, a bold 2–5 word memorable title, and the text at 11 pt.

| Box | Border | Icon (Appendix B) | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Tip:` | 0.75 pt | `tip` (lightbulb); `language` (speech bubble with "Aa") for an ELL tip; `why` for a "why it works" tip | Pedagogical guidance |
| `Feedback:` | 0.75 pt | `feedback` (speech bubble with a check mark); correction scripts begin `Fix:` | Responding to pupil performance |
| `Watch:` | **1.5 pt** (the only heavy box: an alert) | `watch` (an eye with a mark) | A misconception to stay alert for |
| `Research:` | 0.75 pt | `research` (a book) | A critical research finding (Part 5 spec) |

`Note:` is plain text in a script row, never a box. Boxes are black only: purple is reserved for steps.

### 10.6 T-LOOP (data loops)
Three rows; the words carry the meaning, black shapes repeat it:
- solid circle — **`Most:`** → go on;
- half-filled circle — **`About half:`** → a short reteach, then go on;
- hollow circle — **`Fewer than half:`** → stop and reteach with a different, concrete example.
Grid: 5 mm icon · 36 mm bold label (so “Fewer than half:” stays on one line) · text.

### 10.7 T-ICON
No emoji. Black line-art icons (Appendix B): Critical Atom `key`; language barrier `language`; why it
works `why`; manipulative `cubes`; board `board`; mini whiteboard `mwb`. Step numerals and step icons
in teacher scripts are the accent purple (the same marks as the pupil pages).

### 10.8 Colour on teacher pages
Exactly two colours besides black, white and grey, each with the meaning it has on pupil pages and a
second cue: accent #5B2A86 on step numerals and step icons; key #C2410C bold on every answer and expected
response (in scripts, tables and T6). Grey only where a teacher page reproduces a pupil item.

### 10.9 Page by page

| Page | Layout (A4, body 245 mm) |
|---|---|
| **T1 Lesson at a glance** | One 11 pt line: `CCSS … · EE … · CRA: <stage> · Flow: Standard / Chunked`. Band `Lesson guidance:` with ≤ 3 boxes (Tips / one Watch). Band `Lesson flow:` a table (# · Section · Pupil page · Min) of the 9 sections + Transitions + Total = 60. Band `Materials:` a two-column check-box list (5 mm boxes). Band `Vocabulary:` ≤ 3 cards (3 mm rounded, 1.5 pt): mini-diagram, word 13 pt bold, gloss ≤ 8 words. Order fixed: title → standards → guidance → flow → materials → vocabulary. One page. |
| **T2 Atomisation template** | Bands `What students need to know:` (Section 1 table: **# · Skill or Concept · Atom Type · Teacher Notes**; **at least 5 atoms**; types only Category / Comparative / Fact / Conceptual; Pre-requisite rows first, the Atom Type cell reading e.g. `Fact · Pre-requisite`; each Skill cell starts with a bold key name and a colon) · `What students do:` (Section 2 table: Step · Task / Routine atom · Tag · Check it with; tag words `Pre-requisite` / `New`, never ★; Critical Atom row marked with the `key` icon and bold) · `How to teach:` · `Common mistakes:` (Mistake · Broken step · Looks like · Fix) with one line linking each mistake to its Check Question option. Fill to ≥ 92 % of the body (site lint); continue on a 12 mm-header page if it does not fit. |
| **T2b Reference key** | Band `Reference key:` with two columns (1fr 1fr, 4 mm gap) of 0.75 pt tables: page ids (+ packet order and the Score rule), fade levels, data loop shapes, abbreviations, slot shapes (with mini drawings), icons used, band labels. Footer right: `Teacher · Print once per unit`. Tab `Reference`. Filled to ≥ 92 % of the body. |
| **T3–T5 Delivery** | One `Section n · Name (min)` band per section; a `Tip:` / `Watch:` / `Research:` box at its top where useful; then T-SCRIPT rows; a T-LOOP after any check. **Every delivery page shows at least one pupil-page thumbnail**: the real page scaled to 26.2 % (55 × 77.8 mm, 0.75 pt border, no answers) at the right of the first rows of the section that uses that page longest (T3: the Prerequisite Check; T4: the anchor chart and the lesson sheet; T5: Practice 1). More thumbnails only where they fit without a continuation page. Every item reference in a Note line carries its fade label (★ / ★★ / ★★★). T3 = Sections 1–4, T4 = 5–6, T5 = 7–10. A section that does not fit continues on a page with the 12 mm header. |
| **T6 Answer key** | The facsimile key of every pupil page in packet order (each its own page, the anchor chart included), then the routing page: band `Prerequisite routing:` (Question · Prerequisite (T2 atom) · If missed → Lesson), `Accepted answers:` (every item with more than one right answer; one drawn on the key, the rest listed here), `Answers at a glance:` (Page · Answers · **Fade**, one row per page part, Practice split into its ★★ rows and its ★★★ last row), then `Wrong answers to look for when marking` (pupil wrote · likely mistake · next step). |

---

## Part 11 — Output Structure

Generate the pack in this order (one HTML file, then print to PDF):

1. **T1** Lesson at a Glance
2. **T2** Atomisation Template
3. **T2b** Reference Key (print once per unit)
4. **T3** Delivery: Prerequisite Check · Number sense · Spaced retrieval · Chaining Bridge
5. **T4** Delivery: I Do · We Do
6. **T5** Delivery: Check Question · You Do · Word problem · Plenary
7. **Pupil packet** (one master to photocopy): P0 Prerequisite Check · P1 Number sense · P2 Anchor chart ·
   P3 Lesson sheet · P4 Practice 1…n (+ Mixed) · P5 Word problem · P6 Exit slip
8. **T6** Answer keys (facsimiles, in packet order) + routing page

Page counts are targets; never squeeze. A page that does not fit continues (§8.12.4).

---
## Word Problem Framework — Read, Circle, Draw, Write, Solve

The owner's word-problem routine. On paper it is the right-hand **routine panel** of P5 (§9.6): five
check boxes, each with a line-art icon in the accent (no emoji) [AUD C-13, Owner default Q5].

| Step | Panel line (15 pt) | Icon | What pupils do | ELL/SPED support |
|------|------|------|-----------------|------------------|
| **Read it** | Read it two times. | `read` (open book) | Understand the problem | Teacher reads aloud first |
| **Circle it** | Circle the numbers. Underline the question. | `ringnum` | Mark the key information | Teacher models on the board copy |
| **Draw it** | Draw it. | `draw` | Draw a picture of the problem in the `Workspace:` band (≥ 60 mm) | Solid vs hollow counters for the two sets |
| **Write it** | Write the equation. | `write` (pencil) | Fill the equation frame `__ ○ __ = __` | The frame's shape is the scaffold |
| **Solve it** | Solve. Check. | `check` | Write the answer on the line before the printed unit word; say the `Say:` frame | `Say:` “The answer is __ {unit}.” |

One scaffolded problem per page (v1); two faded problems per page (v2: no panel, no word bank). The answer
in words is SAID, never written [P-13].

---

## Scaffolded Math Writing Types

Pupils never compose sentences on paper. Rows marked *oral* are said to a partner and printed on teacher
pages only [P-13, P-LG-4, AUD §2].

| Type | When | Where | Example |
|------|------|--------|--------|
| Fill-in Notes (number blanks only) | I Do / We Do | Pupil page (traced states) | "7 + 2 = [box]" |
| Cloze Sentence (≤ 2 blanks, words from a bank of ≤ 4) | After I Do | Pupil page | "The [box] is the bigger number." bank: start · bigger · count |
| Sentence Frame Response | We Do partner talk | *Oral* — teacher page | “I agree because ___.” |
| Self-Explanation Prompt | I Do → We Do (MANDATORY) | *Oral* — teacher page | “Why did we do Step X before Step Y?” |
| Explain Your Thinking | After Check Question / You Do | *Oral* — teacher page | “I chose C because ___.” |
| Error Analysis | Board only (Find the mistake is paused for print) | *Oral* — teacher page | “The mistake is in the ___. The correct answer is ___.” |
| Word Problem Full Response | You Do — mandatory | Pupil page P5 | RCDWS panel + equation frame + unit line + `Say:` |
| Math Journal Prompt | Plenary | *Oral* | “Today I learned ___.” |

---
## Must-Do Techniques (MANDATORY Every Lesson)
1. Partner talk — tell students what to discuss, name who speaks first, give a time, say go
2. Check for Listening — "Partner B, what did your partner say?"
3. Check for Understanding — scan room, use visualiser/board
4. Cold Call — no hands up; teacher selects
5. Mini whiteboards (MWBs) — "3-2-1 Show Me!"
6. Call and Respond — choral responses for key facts
7. Use of Visualiser/board — show student work publicly

---

## Part 18 — PDF / Image Source Workflow (MANDATORY when any file is uploaded)

When a teacher uploads a PDF or image alongside this prompt, follow every
step below in order before generating any lesson content. Do not skip steps
or combine them. Each step produces output that the next step depends on.

---

### STEP 1 — Read and Understand the Whole Document First

Before identifying standards or extracting problems, read the entire uploaded
file. Do not skim. Understand:

- What mathematical concept or skill is being taught or practised?
- What grade level does the content appear to target?
- Is this a teaching resource (worked examples + practice), a practice-only
  worksheet, an assessment, or a mixed resource?
- Are there any teacher notes, answer keys, or learning objectives already
  printed on the resource? If yes, use them — they are strong evidence for
  the CCSS match.
- What notation and vocabulary does the resource use? Use the same vocabulary
  in the generated lesson (e.g. if the worksheet calls it "regrouping" not
  "borrowing", use "regrouping" throughout).

> Note: If the resource covers more than one skill (e.g. a mixed review
> sheet), identify the PRIMARY skill — the one with the most problems or the
> most instructional space — and build the lesson around that. Mention the
> secondary skills in the Starter questions.

---

### STEP 2 — Identify the CCSS Standard

Match the content to the most specific applicable Common Core State Standards
code. Rules:

- Use the most specific code available. Prefer 3.NF.A.1 over 3.NF.
- If the resource spans two standards, name both and state which is primary.
- If the resource is not clearly aligned (e.g. a publisher worksheet without
  labels), identify the best match based on content and grade-level indicators
  (number size, operation type, vocabulary used).
- State the standard code AND the full standard text on T1 and T3.
- If you cannot determine the grade level from the content alone, state your
  best inference and note it as inferred: e.g. "(inferred Grade 4 based on
  number size and fraction content)"

---


> **V21 changes to this section.** CCSS and EE codes appear only on teacher pages and in the pupil footer [SC-5, HD-30].

### STEP 3 — Write the I Can Statement

Write it as `I Can <verb> <object> (<constraint>)`: "I Can" capitalised, the rest sentence case, the
constraint optional and in brackets, no full stop, at most 60 characters [HD-10]. It is the title of every
page of the pack, teacher pages included [HD-11]. Examples: "I Can add 1–3 (facts to 10)", "I Can subtract
two-digit numbers (no regrouping)".

> **V21 changes to this section.** Method A "EXTRACT verbatim" applies only to the teacher's own material. Third-party (publisher) problems are rewritten in our own words with the same numbers and structure, or used through Method E (the printed sheet), because every page carries the © Cultivating the Digital line [P-33]. **Owner default (2026-10-16, recommended; may change) — Q6.** Method B "KaTeX" becomes "Andika HTML: true glyphs, stacked fractions" (Part 19). Method C SVG follows Appendix D. Method E: the pupil-page reference is a 3 mm rounded box with a 0.75 pt outline and no fill.

### STEP 4 — Classify and Extract Problems

Read every problem in the uploaded resource. For each one, decide which
method below gives the most accurate, most usable result. **Use the
simplest method that works — do not over-engineer.**

**A — EXTRACT verbatim:** The problem text is clean and clear.
Copy the exact wording. Do not paraphrase word problems — the teacher may
be using this resource with students who have already seen it.

**B — RECREATE in Andika HTML:** The problem contains mathematical notation
(fractions, equations, exponents, division symbols, mixed numbers) that
appears as rendered text, image-embedded math, or garbled extraction.
Recreate the problem in Andika HTML with true operator glyphs and stacked fractions (§19.1), so it prints exactly.
Do not try to copy garbled LaTeX or symbol text — recreate from what you
can see.

**C — RECREATE as SVG:** The problem contains a diagram, shape, number line,
bar model, area model, grid, or geometric figure. Recreate it as inline SVG
using the Math Visual Specification in this prompt. Preserve the key features
(dimensions labelled, tick marks, shading) but do not try to pixel-match the
original.

**D — SKIP:** The problem is illegible, ambiguous, or contains an error.
Note the skip and the reason in a comment on the teacher page only.

**E — REFERENCE THE PRINTED PAGE**

**Use ONLY when:** the visual content of the problem — complex shape diagrams,
detailed pictographs, multi-part figures, or highly formatted tables — genuinely
cannot be reproduced accurately using Andika HTML or SVG. This is a faithful-accuracy
decision, not a convenience shortcut. If B or C would produce an accurate
reproduction, use B or C. Only use E when a digital recreation would mislead or
confuse — where the original printed page is clearly more reliable.

**Never use E as a default or first choice.** Attempt B or C first. Use E only
when the attempt would be demonstrably inaccurate.

When E is the right choice:
1. Include the original PDF as a printed handout — teachers print and distribute
   the source worksheet alongside the generated lesson.
2. In the generated lesson, reference the printed page and problem number:

   ```
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │   USE PRINTED SHEET — [Resource name], Page [X]        │
   │  Problem [number or description]                         │
   │  Students complete on the printed sheet.                 │
   │  Teacher: circulate as per the circulation script above. │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   ```

3. On teacher pages: a 3 mm rounded box with a 0.75 pt outline and no fill, label **PRINTED SHEET
   REFERENCE**, page and problem number, plus a one-line description so the
   teacher can find it without hunting.
4. On student pages: grey reference box —
   `See printed sheet — [Resource name] page [X], problem [n]`
   with blank work space below.
5. Include the answer in T6 regardless.

---

**F — MINI WHITEBOARD DELIVERY**

**Use ONLY when:** mini whiteboards genuinely serve the pedagogy of that
specific problem better than paper — typically because the problem is
interactive, collaborative, fast-paced, or designed for live class response
rather than individual written work.

**Appropriate uses of Method F:**
- Starter questions (Section ①) — quick-fire retrieval, read aloud, boards up
- Prerequisite Check tasks (Section ②) — diagnostic, boards up, scan and move
- We Do tasks (Section ④) — guided practice where seeing all boards simultaneously
  tells the teacher whether to continue or reteach
- Specific partner/discussion tasks from activity cards that are explicitly
  designed as verbal prediction or creation activities

**ABSOLUTE RULE — YOU DO (Section ⑥) IS ALWAYS PAPER-BASED:**
Mini whiteboard delivery is NEVER used for the You Do independent practice
section. You Do must always be on paper — either on the generated student
worksheet or on the printed PDF sheet (Method E). Independent practice requires
a written record that the teacher can review during circulation, that students
keep in their books, and that provides accountability for the full practice set.
There are no exceptions to this rule.

**Hybrid model — the most practical use of Methods E and F together:**
A common and highly effective lesson structure when working from a PDF source:

```
Section ④  We Do   → Method F (mini whiteboards)
                     Teacher delivers 2–3 We Do tasks verbally using mini
                     whiteboards. All boards visible simultaneously. No
                     paper needed for this section.

Section ⑥  You Do  → Method E (printed sheet)
                     Students work through the source PDF worksheet
                     independently on paper. Teacher circulates using the
                     V21 circulation script. The generated lesson references
                     the printed sheet by page and problem number.
```

This works because the generated lesson provides the full teaching structure
(T1–T5 delivery, scripts, atomisation, assessment), and the source PDF
provides the independent practice content in its original, accurate format —
without requiring recreation of every problem digitally.

Format Method F tasks on the teacher delivery page exactly like any other
delivery step — full numbered DO / SAY / ASK / NOTE format, with the header:

```
[MINI WHITEBOARD TASK — not on student sheet]
```

---

**Notation decision table — use this every time:**

| What you see in the PDF | What to do |
|---|---|
| Plain word problem, all text | Extract verbatim (A) |
| Number sentence with +, −, ×, ÷ | Extract verbatim (A), use × and ÷ Unicode in HTML |
| Fraction (any form) | Recreate in Andika HTML (§19.1) (B) |
| Mixed number | Recreate in Andika HTML (§19.1) (B) |
| Exponent or square root | Recreate in Andika HTML (§19.1) (B) |
| Equation with variable | Recreate in Andika HTML (§19.1) (B) |
| Number line | Recreate as SVG (C) |
| Bar model / tape diagram | Recreate as SVG (C) |
| Geometric shape with labels | Recreate as SVG (C) |
| Grid / array / area model | Recreate as SVG (C) |
| Clock face | Recreate as SVG (C) |
| Data table | Recreate as HTML table with `.a-table` class |
| Bar chart / pictograph | Recreate as SVG (C) |
| Complex visual that SVG genuinely cannot faithfully reproduce | Reference the printed sheet (E) — only if B/C would be inaccurate |
| Quick verbal question — Starter, Prereq Check, or We Do only | Mini whiteboard delivery (F) — NEVER for You Do |
| Illegible or corrupted | Skip and note (D) |

**Method priority rule:** A > B > C > F > E > D.
Default is always to generate content digitally (A, B, or C). Use mini
whiteboards (F) only where they serve the pedagogy. Use printed sheet reference
(E) only where digital recreation would be inaccurate. Skip (D) only when
genuinely illegible or erroneous.

---

> **V21 changes to this section.** The rows "Check Question = 3-option MC" and "Exit ticket + reflection frame" follow §9 and Part 4: the Check Question is board / mini-whiteboard only; the exit is the 2-up slip (P6) with no written reflection. "Common Student Responses T6b" is removed.

### STEP 5 — Assign Extracted Problems to Lesson Sections

Map each usable problem from the PDF to the correct section of the V21 lesson
structure. Use this logic:

| Problem type in PDF | Where it goes | Permitted methods |
|---|---|---|
| Simplest / most scaffolded problems | ★ You Do (Q1–2) | A, B, C, or E — **never F** |
| Mid-level problems | ★★ You Do (Q3–4) | A, B, C, or E — **never F** |
| Hardest / multi-step problems | ★★★ You Do (Q5+) | A, B, C, or E — **never F** |
| Word problems | Section 6b Word Problem | A, B, or E — **never F** |
| Worked examples | I Do model (Section ③) | A, B, C, or E |
| Quick single-answer checks | Starter ① or Prereq Check ② | A or F |
| Guided practice / partner tasks | We Do ④ | A, B, C, or F |
| Activity cards, discussion tasks, prediction tasks | We Do ④ or Prereq Check ② | F (mini whiteboard) |
| Mastery card with complex visuals that cannot be recreated | You Do ⑥ as printed sheet reference | E only |

**ABSOLUTE RULE — You Do is always paper:**
Every problem in Section ⑥ (You Do) must be completed on paper — either on
the generated student worksheet or on the printed PDF sheet (Method E). Do not
assign any You Do problem as a mini whiteboard task (Method F), regardless of
how the problem was presented in the source material.

**The hybrid model in practice:**
When the source PDF has clear, well-designed practice problems that are
difficult or time-consuming to recreate digitally, the following structure
is recommended:

| Section | Delivery | Source |
|---|---|---|
| ① Starter | Mini whiteboards | Generated (both versions, A and B) |
| ② Prereq Check | Mini whiteboards | Generated |
| ③ I Do | Teacher board | Generated worked examples |
| ④ We Do | Mini whiteboards (F) | From PDF source — verbal delivery |
| ⑤ Check Question | Mini whiteboards | Generated |
| ⑥ You Do | **Printed PDF sheet (E)** | PDF source worksheet — paper only |
| ⑦ Plenary | Mini whiteboards | Generated exit ticket |

This is not a workaround — it is a sound pedagogical structure. The generated
V21 lesson provides the complete teaching sequence. The printed PDF provides
the independent practice. Both work together; neither replaces the other.

**Printed sheet reference format (for teacher page):**
```
 PRINTED SHEET — [Resource name], Page [X], Problems [n–n]
Students work on the printed sheet independently.
Answer key in T6. Circulate using the script above.
```

**Printed sheet reference format (for student page P4):**
```
 See printed sheet — [Resource name], page [X]
Work through problems [n–n] independently.
Show your work on the printed sheet.
```

**Mini whiteboard format (for teacher page — We Do only):**
```
[MINI WHITEBOARD TASK — not on student sheet]
```
Write as a full numbered delivery step with DO / SAY / ASK / NOTE labels.

If the PDF has more problems than the lesson needs, prioritise:
1. Problems that exactly match the CCSS standard
2. Problems that vary one feature at a time (best for We Do)
3. Multi-step or cross-topic problems (best for ★★★ You Do)
4. Remaining problems — set aside; note in T6 as "additional practice from source"

If the PDF has fewer problems than the lesson needs → see Step 6.

---

### STEP 6 — Identify and Create Missing Elements

A worksheet or textbook page almost never contains everything V21 requires.
After assigning extracted problems, check every required element against the
V21 structure and create anything missing. This is mandatory — the PDF
content drives what problems students practise, but V21 requirements still
govern what the lesson must contain.

**Elements almost always missing from uploaded worksheets — create all of these:**

| Missing element | Where it goes | What to create |
|---|---|---|
| Teacher delivery instructions | T3–T5 | Full numbered DO/SAY/ASK/NOTE steps for every section |
| Prerequisite Check questions | T3 ② | 2–3 mini whiteboard tasks checking building-block skills |
| I Do worked example(s) | T4 ③ | 2–3 worked examples with visual models, using same numbers/context as PDF where possible |
| Wrong example (most common mistake) | T4 ③ step 5 | Show the most common mistake for this skill |
| We Do tasks | T4 ④ | 2–3 tasks at ★ and ★★ if not present in PDF |
| Check Question | T5 ⑤ | One 3-option multiple-choice question; wrong answers match named mistakes from T2 |
| Differentiated Starter | T3 ① | 6-question spaced retrieval starter (both versions) |
| ELL sentence frames | T4 ④ | One fill-in-the-blank frame per We Do talk task |
| Word problem | P5 / T5 ⑥b | If PDF has no word problem, create one at ★★ level in same context |
| Visual models (Dual Coding) | T4 ③ | SVG model alongside every I Do worked example |
| Exit slip | T5 Section 10 / P6 | One problem + `Say:` frame + two check boxes (no written reflection) |
| Consolidation scripts | T5 | Verbatim scripts for word problem debrief and plenary |
| Answer key | T6 | Full answers for all sections including PDF problems |
| Common Student Responses | T6b | 4–6 embedded tip boxes based on named common mistakes for this skill |
| Top Tips | T1 | 3–5 lesson-specific tips, each with a memorable positive title |

> Note: A worksheet that only has practice problems gives you the You Do
> section. Everything else — the entire teaching sequence, all delivery scripts,
> all scaffolding, all ELL support, the assessment structures — must be created
> from scratch. This is the main value of V21 applied to an uploaded resource:
> the PDF provides the content, V21 provides the complete lesson.

---

### STEP 7 — Quality Check Before Outputting

Before generating the lesson, verify:

☐ Every problem extracted from the PDF is mathematically correct as rendered.
☐ Every recreated expression uses true glyphs (−, ×, ÷) and stacked fractions.
☐ Every SVG recreation matches the original diagram in all key features.
☐ Every printed sheet reference (Method E) was chosen because digital recreation would be inaccurate — not for convenience.
☐ Every printed sheet reference includes: resource name, page number, problem number, and answer in T6.
☐ Every mini whiteboard task (Method F) is in Section ①, ②, or ④ only — never in Section ⑥ You Do.
☐ Every mini whiteboard task is formatted as a numbered delivery step — not placed on the student sheet.
☐ Section ⑥ You Do contains only paper-based problems — on the generated student worksheet or on a referenced printed sheet.
☐ The CCSS standard matches the content of the PDF — not just the grade level.
☐ The I Can statement matches both the CCSS and the specific PDF content.
☐ All V21 mandatory requirements are present (use the Mandatory Rules section as a checklist).
☐ Problems from the PDF are used verbatim or recreated faithfully — no accidental paraphrasing.
☐ Any skipped problems (Method D) are noted on the teacher page with a reason.
☐ T6 Answer Key includes answers for ALL problems — generated, recreated, referenced by page, or delivered on mini whiteboards.
☐ The teacher can teach the full lesson using only the generated pages plus any noted printed sheets — no missing information.

---


**V21 print checks (add to the quality check):** run Appendix E. In short: A4; Andika on every node; three paints plus the accent on step marks and the key colour on answers; no emoji; B(n) slots; facsimile keys; © line and strand tab on every page; every page ≥ 50 % used; no cell band ≥ 30 % empty.

### PDF Problem Method — Quick Decision Guide

Run through these questions in order. Stop at the first that applies.

**First — always ask: is this a You Do problem?**
If the problem belongs in Section ⑥ (You Do / independent practice), it must
be paper-based. Go straight to A, B, C, or E. Method F is not available here.

**Then for all other sections:**

| Question | If YES | Method |
|---|---|---|
| Is it plain text with no special notation? | Use it exactly as written | A — Extract verbatim |
| Does it contain fractions, exponents, or equation notation? | Recreate in Andika HTML | B — Andika HTML |
| Does it contain a number line, shape, grid, bar model, or chart? | Recreate as SVG | C — SVG |
| Is this a Starter, Prereq Check, or We Do task best done with all boards visible simultaneously? | Deliver on mini whiteboards | F — Mini whiteboard (Starter, Prereq, We Do only) |
| Is the visual too complex or inaccurate to recreate with B or C? | Reference the printed page | E — Printed sheet (accuracy failure only) |
| Is the problem illegible, broken, or contains an error? | Skip it with a note | D — Skip |

**Method priority: A > B > C > F > E > D**

**Two conditions that trigger E or F:**
- Use E only when: B or C would produce an inaccurate or misleading result
- Use F only when: the problem genuinely works better as a live class activity than as paper, AND it is not in Section ⑥

Every other situation uses A, B, or C.

---

Copy this into any generated lesson's teacher notes as needed:

```
FRACTION:      $\frac{3}{4}$           → ¾ (typeset)
MIXED NUMBER:  $2\frac{1}{3}$          → 2⅓ (typeset)
MULTIPLY:      $6 \times 4 = 24$       → 6 × 4 = 24
DIVIDE:        $24 \div 4 = 6$         → 24 ÷ 4 = 6
SQUARED:       $5^2 = 25$              → 5² = 25
SQRT:          $\sqrt{25} = 5$         → √25 = 5
PERCENTAGE:    $35\%$                  → 35%
DEGREES:       $90°$                   → 90°
BLANK:         $\_\_\_$                → ___
LESS THAN:     $3 < 5$                 → 3 < 5
GREATER THAN:  $5 > 3$                 → 5 > 3
APPROX:        $\approx 4$             → ≈ 4
```

---

**Format:** User's requested format. Default HTML. Never override.

**Spacing Effect (Internal Only — NEVER label in output):** Plan Q1–Q5 across
temporal distances (recent → distant). Never label these on any generated
material.

**Interleaving (Internal Only — NEVER label in output):** Q1–Q5 each from a
different math domain; exactly five domains across Q1–Q5. Domain labels NEVER
appear on student sheets. Domain labels MAY appear in small grey text beside
questions on teacher delivery pages only.

**Page Count:** Aim for Teacher View = 7 pages (T1, T2, T2b, T3, T4, T5, T6)
and Pupil packet = P0–P6 (§9). These are targets, not hard limits.
Add pages freely when content needs space. Sections may continue across pages.
Content must never be cut off or squeezed to fit a page target.
The Lesson at a Glance (T1) is always the first teacher page.
T6 (Answer Key) is always the final teacher page.
Correction tip boxes are embedded within T3–T5 — no standalone T6b page.

- Answer key: facsimile keys, answers in #C2410C Andika 700 in the pupil's slots (§8.12.7).
page T6. Footer: `TEACHER USE ONLY — DO NOT DISTRIBUTE TO STUDENTS`.

---

## Teacher page content specifications (PART 0–5)

Layout and type for every part below: Part 10. This part specifies what each teacher page says.

### PART 0: Lesson at a Glance (Teacher Page T1)

This is the first page of teacher materials. It is the only page a teacher
needs to read before walking into the room. Keep it dense and scannable.
Layout and type: §10.9 (T1 row) and §8.3. **Content order — top to bottom, never reorder:**

```
| lifted header: I Can title (18 pt bold, the pack's one title)        [Level n / strand / Teacher 1] |
|=====================================================================================================|
| CCSS 1.OA.C.6 · EE M.EE.2.NBT.7 · CRA: Pictures · Flow: Standard            <- one 11 pt line       |
+=====================================================================================================+
| Lesson guidance:                                                                                    |
|  (Tip: Big first, every time. ...)   <- up to 3 boxes (Tip / Watch), 2-5 word bold titles           |
+=====================================================================================================+
| Lesson flow:   table  # · Section · Pupil page · Min   (9 sections + Transitions + Total 60)         |
+=====================================================================================================+
| Materials:     [ ] item   [ ] item     <- two columns, 5 mm check boxes                             |
+=====================================================================================================+
| Vocabulary:    (+ plus ...) (= equals ...) (count on ...)   <- <= 3 cards, mini-diagram + gloss    |
```

**Visual hierarchy rules (enforce this order — never reorder):**
1. Title (the shared 18 pt I Can title)
2. Standards line — compact, one 11 pt line (CCSS code only here; the full text goes on T3)
3. `Lesson guidance:` + Tips — always above Lesson flow
4. `Lesson flow:`
5. `Materials:`
6. `Vocabulary:`

---
> **V21 changes to this section.** Tip titles keep their rules. Emoji prefixes are replaced by the black line-art icons `tip`, `language`, `why` (or no icon); the amber box is the `Tip:` box of §10.5. Label: `Tip:` (not "LESSON TIP").

#### Top Tips for Teaching Success — Specification

**Two tiers of tips — different purpose, different location:**

---

**TIER 1 — Whole-Lesson Tips on T1 (1–3 tips maximum)**

These are the only tips on T1. They are strategic lesson-level watchouts — things a teacher
needs to hold in mind for the *entire* lesson, not specific to one section. A teacher with
five minutes reads these and nothing else before walking in.

**What belongs here:** Big-picture conceptual insights — the Critical Atom, the key
vocabulary barrier for ELL students across the whole lesson, and the research rationale
behind the lesson's structure. These apply everywhere, not at one specific moment.

**What does NOT belong here:** Mistake prevention, section-specific moves, circulation
scripts, We Do tips, I Do techniques. Those belong in the sections where they occur.

**Maximum 3 tips.** If a fourth would be a whole-lesson insight, include it. If it is
section-specific, move it to that section instead.

Render as the `Tip:` box (§10.5).

**Required T1 tip content:**

1. **[[Critical Atom] Memorable phrase — the Critical Atom]**
   The one step that determines whether the whole lesson succeeds or fails. Name it and
   give the single image or question that makes it click. Teachers carry this all lesson.

2. **[ Memorable phrase — the ELL/vocabulary barrier]**
   The single word or phrase that is the biggest language barrier throughout the lesson.
   What to say, show, or draw when it becomes a wall. Applies in every section.

3. **[ Memorable phrase — why the lesson is structured this way]** *(optional)*
   The one research finding or structural insight that explains the lesson design.
   Only include if genuinely lesson-level — skip if it applies to only one section.

---

**TIER 2 — Section Teaching Tips (embedded at the top of each delivery section)**

All other tips live at the top of the section where they are needed. The teacher opens
T4 for the I Do and immediately sees the tip for that moment — no memory required, no
flipping back to T1.

**Format — Section Tip box:**

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│   SECTION TIP: [Memorable 2–5 word phrase]                 │
│  [One or two sentences — specific, actionable, lesson-         │
│   specific. Tells the teacher exactly what to watch for or   │
│   do in THIS section.]                                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Render as the `Tip:` box (§10.5): 3 mm rounded, 0.75 pt black outline, no fill.
Placed at the top of the section, above the delivery steps. One per section maximum.
Use the same memorable-title format as T1 tips — action phrase, not category label.

**Where Section Tips go and what they cover:**

| Section | What the Section Tip should address |
|---------|-------------------------------------|
| ① Starter | What to look for when scanning boards; the most telling retrieval gap |
| ② Prereq Check | The specific prerequisite most likely to be shaky; reteach move if it is |
| ②b Chaining Bridge | Which two atoms to name explicitly; the sentence to say |
| ③ I Do | The teaching move that unlocks this content; orientation/variation reminder |
| ④ We Do | The most common We Do mistake; the sentence frame that unlocks ELL responses |
| ⑤ Check Question | What the wrong answers reveal; which mistake to address first |
| ⑥ You Do | Circulation priority; the process question for the most common mistake |
| ⑦ Plenary | What to listen for in the reflection; who to check in with tomorrow |

Not every section needs a tip. Generate one only where there is something genuinely
useful to say for this lesson. Skip sections where no specific watchout exists.

**Title format for ALL tips (T1 and Section):**
Every tip must begin with a **bold memorable phrase (2–5 words)** — a slogan or move
name, not a category label. The phrase captures the insight in a way a teacher remembers.

**Banned titles (generic category labels):**
"The hardest moment:", "ELL/SPED critical support:", "The move that unlocks:",
"Stop the mistake:", "The research reason:", "Watch for:". Replace every one of these
with something specific to this lesson's content.

**Example comparison:**

| Bad (banned generic label) | Good (memorable, lesson-specific) |
|---|---|
| The hardest moment: | [Critical Atom] "Trace to the Wall!" |
| Stop the most common mistake: |  "More Means Gap, Not Sum" |
| The move that unlocks this lesson: |  "Flip the Factors First" |
| ELL/SPED critical support: |  "The Word Is 'Difference'" |
| Why we do Dual Coding: |  "Two Traces Beat One" |
| Watch for in We Do: |  "They'll Add — Show the Gap" |

---

### PART 1: Atomisation Template (Teacher Page T2)

Pre-lesson planning page. Consult before the lesson. Not needed during
delivery. Contains five sections in sequence on one page.

---

#### Key Vocabulary

Render as vocabulary cards in a row (3 per row): each a 3 mm rounded box with a 1.5 pt outline, holding a
mini-diagram (an arrow to the thing named, or the symbol itself), the word in bold 13 pt, and a plain
gloss of 8 words or fewer at 11 pt [P-LG-7]. Minimum 3 words, maximum 6 on T2. The ≤ 3 terms that reach
the pupil page (P3 `Vocabulary:`) are marked with a 5 mm check box beside the word. Black only.

```
( [mini-diagram]  )  ( [mini-diagram]  )  ( [mini-diagram]  )
(  [x] Word 1     )  (  [x] Word 2     )  (  [ ] Word 3     )
(  gloss <= 8 w.  )  (  gloss <= 8 w.  )  (  gloss <= 8 w.  )
```

---
> **V21 changes to this section.** Tables per §10.4: 0.75 pt rules, bold black head row under a 1.5 pt rule, no fills or zebra rows. Atom types, step names and mistake names are bold black sentence case (for example **Zero drop**), never blue, never ALL CAPS. The Critical Atom is marked with the `key` icon.

#### Section 1 — Conceptual Atomisation

**What students need to know** — the knowledge, understanding, and decisions
that underpin the routine. Routine atoms do NOT go here.

Atom type key (small text above table):
*Atom type reminder: Category = yes-or-no decision | Comparative = spot a change |
Fact = recall something | Conceptual = understand why |
(Task and Routine atoms go in Section 2)*

**Rules:**
- Prerequisites listed first and tagged "Pre-requisite"
- Minimum 5 atoms
- Each atom statable in one sentence
- No Task or Routine atoms in this table — those go in Section 2
- Every pre-requisite atom must appear in the Starter or Prerequisite Check

**Formatting rules for Skill or Concept column:**
Bold the key concept name at the start, followed by a colon, then the sentence.
Example: **Decompose:** A 2-digit number is made of tens and ones.
Example: **Tens Multiplication:** Multiplying a multiple of 10 by a multiple of 10 makes hundreds.

**Formatting rules for Atom Type column:**
Render atom types in bold black (§10.4).

| # | Skill or Concept | Atom Type | Teacher Notes |
|---|-----------------|-----------|---------------|
| 1 | **[Key Name]:** [one-sentence description] | Category / Comparative / Fact / Conceptual · Pre-requisite or New | Pre-requisite or New. What to watch for. |

---

#### Section 2 — Routine Atomisation

**What students need to be able to do** — every single discrete action in the
procedure, broken to the smallest possible level. Each row is one action a
student takes: one prompt, one response, one thing to check. This is NOT the
teaching sequence — it is the student's execution sequence.

Mark the Critical Atom [Critical Atom] — the one atom that determines whether the whole
routine succeeds or fails.

**Rules:**
- Each row = one discrete action only. Two things in one row = split it.
- Number rows sequentially (1, 2, 3…). No letter prefixes — write the step number only.
- Each action begins with a **bold** key verb: **Decompose**, **Draw**, **Multiply**, **Add**, etc.
  Bold the first action word in the student action description only — not the full sentence.
  Example: **Decompose** both numbers into tens and ones.
  Example: **Draw** a 2×2 box and label the top and left sides.
- Nothing implied or grouped — every invisible step is visible
- Task and Routine atoms belong here (not in Section 1)

**Formatting rules for Type column:**
Render atom types in bold black (§10.4).

| Step | The action — what the student does | Teacher prompt | Type | Example |
|------|-------------------------------------|---------------|------|---------|
| 1 [Critical Atom] | **[Key verb]** [rest of action] | "[Exact prompt]" | Task / Routine | [What correct execution looks like] |

---

#### Section 3 — How to Teach

**The instructional sequence** — what the teacher does to deliver the routine
atoms into students. Each teaching step groups one or more student steps into a
single teachable moment. Teaching steps have memorable verb names.
This is the teacher's plan, not the student's execution sequence.

**Formatting rules for Name column:**
Render teaching step names in bold black (§10.4).
Example: `<td><span class="step-name">Split & Frame</span></td>`
Example: `<td><span class="step-name">Multiply Boxes</span></td>`

| Teaching Step | Name | What the teacher says or does | What students do | Student steps covered |
|--------------|------|-------------------------------|-----------------|----------------------|
| 1 | `<span class="step-name">[Verb name]</span>` | [Teacher action or prompt] | [Student response] | Steps [n] |

---

#### Section 4 — Common Mistakes

Each mistake has a short ALL-CAPS name and a plain-English description.
The "Broken step" column names the student step number where the mistake occurs.
Every mistake here must match one wrong answer option in the Check Question on T5.
Write the mistake name in plain English — no letter codes.

**Formatting rules for Mistake Name column:**
Render mistake names in bold black (§10.4).
Example: `<td><span class="mistake-name">ZERO DROP</span></td>`
Example: `<td><span class="mistake-name">ADD INSTEAD</span></td>`

| Mistake name | What the mistake is | What it looks like | Broken step | What to say to the student |
|-------------|--------------------|--------------------|-------------|--------------------------|
| `<span class="mistake-name">[SHORT NAME IN CAPS]</span>` | [Description] | [Worked example showing the error] | Step [n] | "[Exact script to say]" |

---

> **V21 changes to this section.** Same table treatment as T2. The Check Question is shown on the board and mini whiteboards (never printed for pupils). Print once per unit; tab id `Reference`.

### PART 2: Reference Key (Teacher Page T2b)

**PLANNING REFERENCE ONLY — print once per unit, not with each lesson.**
Not needed during live delivery. Two-column layout.

**Formatting rules for T2b (bold key elements throughout):**
- Student step action verbs: bold the first verb in each step.
  Example: **Decompose** both numbers. | **Draw** 2×2 grid and label.
- Atom Type names: Render these in bold black (§10.4).
- Teaching step names: Render these in bold black (§10.4).
- Mistake names: Render these in bold black (§10.4).
- Abbreviation terms (left column): render in bold.
  Example: **MWBs** | **RCDWS** | **ELL/SPED**

**Left column:**

1. **Star Ratings**

| Symbol | Name | What it means |
|--------|------|--------------|
| ★ | Supported | Completion problem + text hint. Every student starts here. |
| ★★ | Independent | Final step blank only. The main goal. Every student must reach this. |
| ★★★ | Challenge | Fully blank. No scaffold. Multi-step. Fully independent. |

2. **Student Steps (Routine Atomisation)**
One row per step from T2 Section 2. Bold the first action verb in each step.

| Step | Single student action |
|------|-----------------------|
| 1 [Critical Atom] | **[Key verb]** [rest of action from T2] |

3. **Teaching Steps (How to Teach)**
Step names in bold black (§10.4).

| Teaching Step | Name | Student steps it covers |
|--------------|------|------------------------|
| 1 | `<span class="step-name">[Name]</span>` | Steps [n] |

**Right column:**

4. **Common Mistakes**
Mistake names in bold black (§10.4).

| Mistake name | What to watch for |
|-------------|-------------------|
| `<span class="mistake-name">[SHORT NAME]</span>` | [One-line description] |

5. **All 6 Atom Types — What they are and how to teach them**

| Atom Type | Plain English | How to teach it |
|------|--------------|-----------------|
| Category | A yes-or-no decision. "Is this a ___?" Includes recognising vocabulary and invisible decisions. | Show examples and non-examples on the board — one thing changes at a time. Students vote yes/no on mini whiteboards. NPPPN order: Wrong → Right → Right (different) → Right (very different) → Wrong. |
| Comparative | Spotting change — something gets bigger, smaller, faster. "What happened?" | Show before-and-after pairs. PPNN order: two right, two wrong, one feature varying each time. Mini whiteboards. |
| Fact | Something to remember — a formula, rule, or property. | Call and Respond: teacher says it → frames it → reframes it → students rehearse with a partner. |
| Task | A single, specific action or calculation as part of a larger process — simplify, rearrange, read a value. *(Older Barton materials call this "Transformation" — always write "Task".)* | 1–3 examples on the board, changing one feature each time. Testing sequence on mini whiteboards from simple to complex. |
| Routine | A multi-step process where the result is not immediately obvious. | Full I Do (silent model + narration) → We Do → You Do. Cannot be shortcutted. |
| Conceptual | Understanding *why* a step or rule works. | 60-second private WHY write on mini whiteboards. Deliberate WHY questions during We Do. Exit reflection. |

6. **Abbreviations**

| Short form | Full form | What it means in practice |
|---|---|---|
| MWB / MWBs | mini whiteboards | Small dry-erase boards so the teacher can see every student's answer at once. |
| ELL | English language learners | Students developing English proficiency. May need sentence frames, visual support, and vocabulary scaffolds. |
| SPED | special education students | Students with an individual education plan or identified support needs. May need a times table chart, reduced number of problems, or enlarged print. |
| RCDWS | Read, Circle, Draw, Write, Solve | The five-step word problem framework. Pupil band labels:      |
| CRA | Concrete-Representational-Abstract | Learning sequence: physical objects → diagrams and drawings → numbers and symbols. |
| CCSS | Common Core State Standards | US national curriculum framework for mathematics and English. |

7. **Lesson Structure Terms**

| Term | What it means |
|---|---|
| Teacher model | Teacher works through an example silently first, then explains aloud. Students watch. |
| Partner talk | Students explain thinking to a partner for a set time before Cold Call. |
| Check Question | One multiple-choice question shown to the whole class after guided practice. Tells the teacher whether to reteach or move on. Each wrong answer matches a named common mistake. |
| Show Call | Teacher places a student's work under the visualiser/board for class discussion. Work shown anonymously if it contains an error. |
| Word problem steps | Read it → Circle it → Draw it → Write it → Solve it and check |
| Instruction sequence | The short, near-silent teacher demonstration used to introduce a Category, Comparative, Fact, or Task atom. |
| Testing sequence | The mini-whiteboard practice that immediately follows the instruction sequence to check whether students understood. |
| Chaining Bridge | Verbally connecting 2–3 familiar atoms before the full routine begins (Section ②b). |
| Critical Atom [Critical Atom] | The single most important atom in the routine — the one that, if understood, determines success. Usually a Category or Conceptual atom. |


---

### PART 3: The Lesson Plan (Teacher Pages T3–T5)

---

#### Lesson Plan Header (top of T3)

1. The shared I Can title (in the header).
2. Standard line, plain 11 pt: `CCSS 1.OA.C.6 · EE M.EE.2.NBT.7` plus the full CCSS text once.
3. Flow line: `Flow: Standard` or `Flow: Chunked`.
4. Materials (check-box list): mini whiteboards + markers; visualiser / board; **pupil lesson packet**
   (Prerequisite Check, Number sense, Anchor chart, Lesson sheet, Practice ×n, Word problem, Exit slip);
   manipulatives; any lesson-specific materials.

**V21 section numbering on T3–T5:** 1 Prerequisite Check · 2 Number sense · 3 Spaced retrieval · 4 Chaining
Bridge · 5 I Do · 6 We Do · 7 Check Question · 8 You Do · 9 Word problem · 10 Plenary. The V20 section
specifications below keep their content; their V20 names are given in brackets.

#### Section 1a — Number Sense Warm-Up (5 Minutes)

> **V21 changes to this section.** Now **Section 2 · Number sense (5 min)**, pupil page P1 (§9.2, Owner default Q4). Both bands print as production slots in bands A / B; the V20 two-column layout is gone. Teacher answers in the key colour.

**What it is.** Five number-sense items in two bands on pupil page P1 (§9.2): band A (numbers 2–4 grades
below) and band B (the same five types, numbers 1–2 grades below). Drawn from the Number Sense Practice Bank,
rotating categories across the week; Q1 is always a counting or skip-counting item.

**Teacher page format (T3, Section 2):**
```
Section 2 · Number sense (5 min)
 (1) Do:   Pupils do band A (1-5). Early finishers do band B (6-10).
     Say:  “Point to the box. Say the number. Then write it.”
 (2) Do:   Choral check: read each item and its answer together.
     Note: A: <answers in the key colour> · B: <answers in the key colour>
```
Rotation note (teacher page only): which categories have not appeared this week.

**Delivery steps:** 1. Hand out P1. 2. Read band A row 1 aloud; pupils write. 3. Pupils continue alone;
circulate (ELL / SPED first). 4. Pupils who finish A start B. 5. Choral check of A, then B. Every answer is a
production slot (box, line, circle); nothing is multiple choice.

#### Section 1b — Spaced Retrieval (5 Minutes)

> **V21 changes to this section.** Now **Section 3 · Spaced retrieval (3 min)**: mini whiteboards only, nothing printed (Owner default Q3). Domain labels print as plain 11 pt words in brackets on the teacher page (grey is never used for words). Option B is 1–2 grades below, as in the bank table.

**What it is.** Three quick cross-domain questions on **mini whiteboards only — nothing is printed**
(Owner default Q3). Earlier topics only, one per domain, 1–2 grades below the lesson (Option B of the bank
table). Domain labels appear on the teacher page only, as plain 11 pt words in brackets.

**Teacher page format (T3, Section 3):**
```
Section 3 · Spaced retrieval (3 min, mini whiteboards)
 (1) Ask:  “<question 1>”  <answer in the key colour>   [domain]
     Ask:  “<question 2>”  <answer>                      [domain]
     Ask:  “<question 3>”  <answer>                      [domain]
```
**Delivery:** read each question, count silently to 5, “3-2-1, show me”, scan every board, name the most
common wrong answer without naming the pupil, then the next question.

#### Section 2 — Prerequisite Check (3 Minutes)

> **V21 changes to this section.** Now **Section 1 · Prerequisite Check (4 min)**, the printed page P0 (§9.1): 3–4 items, most basic first, each tagged on the key "If missed → Lesson …". Run it "3-2-1, show me" in the same order. The data loop uses T-LOOP shapes; the routing table is on T6 (§8.12.8).


> **SECTION TIP PLACEMENT:** Render a `.section-tip` box at the top of this section.
> Draw from the library: **1.4 Replace Self-Report, 1.14 Hinge Questions, 1.2 Show Me,
> 1.12 Culture of Error, 1.8 Affirmative Checking, 4.10 Excavate Error, 1.15 Responsive Teaching.**
> Address the specific prerequisite most likely to be shaky AND the reteach move —
> almost always worth including for this section.
> (e.g.  "Zero Is a Placeholder, Not Nothing — reteach with a place-value chart").

**Format:** 2–3 short tasks on mini whiteboards.
checks one building block that students need before the main lesson. Include
at least one yes/no question.

Each task must appear on the teacher delivery page with:
- The exact question to ask
- The correct answer (in the key colour #C2410C, bold)
- What to look for on the boards
- Which building block it checks (from T2 Section 1)

**Delivery steps:**

1.  DO: Keep mini whiteboards out from the Starter.
    SAY: “Now I need to check two things before we move on. Mini whiteboards ready.”

2.  DO: Read Task 1 aloud. Point to it on the visualiser/board if helpful.
    SAY: “[Task 1 question].”
    DO: Count silently to 5.
    SAY: “Show me in 3, 2, 1.”

    > Note: Correct answer for Task 1 is shown in the key colour on this page.
    > If more than 3 students show a wrong answer → go to step 3a.
    > If most boards are correct → go to step 3b.

3a. DO: Stay at the board. Use a completely different example.
    SAY: “Let me show you this one more time. Watch.”
    [Reteach script — lesson-specific, written here verbatim.]
    DO: Give one fresh mini whiteboard question to recheck.

3b. SAY: “Good. Next one.”
    DO: Repeat step 2 for Task 2 (and Task 3 if included).

**PREREQ CHECK DATA LOOP — scan boards after each task and pick one:**

(hollow circle) Fewer than half: **Fewer than half correct:**
    DO: Do NOT proceed to the I Do yet.
    SAY: “Let me show you this one more time — different example.”
    DO: Reteach with a completely new example. Model silently → narrate briefly → one more
    mini whiteboard question. Scan again. If still below half, note which students need
    support and proceed — but visit those students within the first 60 seconds of You Do.

(half circle) About half: **About half correct:**
    SAY: “A few people got a different answer. Watch this part.”
    DO: Name and show the specific error pattern (one sentence, one example). One more quick
    mini whiteboard question before moving on.

(solid circle) Most: **Most correct:**
    SAY: “Good.”
    DO: Move straight to the Chaining Bridge (Section ②b) or directly to the I Do.

    > Note: The same data-loop logic as the Check Question applies here. A 60-second
    > catch here prevents 20 minutes of confusion in the You Do. Do not skip it.

Numbered circle: ②

---

#### Section ②b — Chaining Bridge (1 Minute)

> **V21 changes to this section.** Now **Section 4 · Chaining Bridge (1 min)**.


**What this is:** A brief, named moment between the Prerequisite Check and the I Do where the
teacher verbally connects two or three atoms the students have just demonstrated, before the
full routine is shown. Students who can do each atom separately still freeze when they see the
full procedure — the Chaining Bridge prevents that freeze by making the connection explicit.

**This step is mandatory when the routine has 3 or more atoms, and essential when using
